Hi, doctor. Um, thank you so much for fitting me in, I really appreciate it. I hope I'm not wasting your time or anything. I guess I'm just here because I've been having a bit of a hard time lately with... well, just sitting. 

I mean, it's probably not a huge deal, but I'm in school right now to be a radiologic tech, and whenever I'm supposed to just sit quietly, like in a lecture or in the radiology suite, I get this really intense feeling like I'm... I still kind of get that restless feeling. \n\nBut I try really hard to hide it! Like, I don't want to be annoying to anyone. I really struggle to just sit and relax. I don't want you to think I'm, you know, overly anxious or stressed out. \n\nIt's really just a physical feeling in my body. Like my muscles just want to go. My mind is usually pretty clear, or at least, I'm just trying to focus on whatever I'm supposed to be doing, like paying attention to the instructor. I just... my body just won't cooperate. Does that make sense? I hope I'm answering that right. I just don't really get those kinds of unstoppable thoughts or worries with it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel upset or frustrated when you're experiencing this restless feeling?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I guess... It's just a little frustrating when I'm trying so hard to be professional and do the right thing, and my body just won't listen. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... periods where I don't feel restless at all? Gosh, I... I don't think so? I really hope it's okay that I'm saying that. I don't want to sound like I'm exaggerating or being difficult or anything! I try really hard to just push through it and be a good student in my radiology program. 

But honestly, it just feels like it's always there. Even when I'm trying to relax or go to sleep, my brain and my body just feel like they need to be doing something. I mean, I guess when I'm actually asleep I don't feel it, but other than that... no, not really.

If I need to try harder to find moments where I'm completely still, I absolutely will! I just want to make sure I'm doing what you need me to do.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, careless mistakes? Gosh, I really hope not! I mean, especially with the radiology stuff, details are just so important, you know? I try to be so, so careful with everything I do. 

I really don't think I have a problem with missing details. I mean, I always try to double-check my work because I really want to do a good job and impress my instructors.
